<micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>

From micron reference
micron reference › Implementation notes

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

This is an implementation header. Some named declarations are exposed through public headers; include the public interface that provides the facility.

Function overloads are documented below with their declarations, source-equivalent effects, return sites, and explicit failure paths. Read the complete source snapshot for the surrounding type definitions and macros.

Type reference

micron::gfx::vk::VkBaseInStructure

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Supplies the tag and extension-chain prefix used by raw Vulkan records.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkBaseInStructure, VkBaseOutStructure, VkStructureTypeInput/output chain prefix with sType and pNext; VkStructureType selects the concrete record layout
VkApplicationInfo, VkInstanceCreateInfoApplication/API-version metadata and instance layer/extension requests
VkDeviceQueueCreateInfo, VkDeviceCreateInfo, VkDeviceQueueInfo2Queue-family priorities and device feature/extension creation inputs; protected-queue lookup input
VkExtensionProperties, VkLayerPropertiesEnumeration outputs with inline name/description arrays and version fields
VkDevicePrivateDataCreateInfo, VkPrivateDataSlotCreateInfoDevice private-data capacity request and private-data slot creation input

Requirements and lifetime

These are plain ABI records with no owning constructors. Zero initialization does not in general supply the required sType, nonzero limits or valid native defaults. structure_type_of supplies selected tag constants but does not initialize the record. Input pNext chains must contain permitted live records; output chains must contain correctly tagged writable storage.

Count/pointer pairs, names and nested feature arrays borrow caller storage for the applicable native call. No record copies pointed-to arrays, negotiates enabled features or retains an instance/device. Generated VK_STRUCTURE_TYPE_* aliases denote typed tag constants, including selected extension spellings, rather than runtime feature probes.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkExtent2D

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Supplies integer regions, image subresource selectors, component mappings and clear-value storage.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkOffset2D, VkOffset3D, VkExtent2D, VkExtent3D, VkRect2D, VkClearRectSigned offsets, unsigned extents, 2D render rectangles and layer-qualified clear rectangles
VkViewportFloating viewport position/size and minimum/maximum depth
VkComponentMappingPer-component image-view swizzle selectors
VkImageSubresource, VkImageSubresourceLayers, VkImageSubresourceRangeAspect plus mip/layer coordinates or ranges; the operation selects which form is required
VkClearColorValue, VkClearDepthStencilValue, VkClearValue, VkClearAttachmentFloat/signed/unsigned color union views, depth/stencil values, and clear attachment selection

Requirements and lifetime

Region records store their scalar fields inline and allocate nothing. Width/height/depth, offsets, level/layer counts and aspect masks must describe valid ranges for the target operation. A nonzero extent does not establish valid storage or layout.

Color and general clear unions carry no dynamic discriminant. The attachment format and native operation determine the active member. Assigning a different union view is not a numeric conversion of the stored representation. References to these values remain tied to their enclosing record/array lifetime.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkPhysicalDeviceProperties

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes physical-device capabilities and extensible feature/property query outputs.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkPhysicalDeviceProperties, VkPhysicalDeviceLimits, VkPhysicalDeviceSparseProperties, VkPhysicalDeviceProperties2Core identity, numeric limits and sparse capabilities, with an extensible properties wrapper
VkPhysicalDeviceFeatures, VkPhysicalDeviceFeatures2, VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan11Features, VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan12Features, VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan13FeaturesCore and version-grouped feature Booleans for query or explicit device enablement
VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan11Properties, VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan12Properties, VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan13PropertiesVersion-grouped subgroup, memory, descriptor, floating-point and other implementation limits
VkQueueFamilyProperties, VkQueueFamilyProperties2, VkPhysicalDeviceMemoryProperties, VkPhysicalDeviceMemoryProperties2, VkMemoryType, VkMemoryHeapQueue flags/count/granularity and bounded native memory-type/heap inventories
VkFormatProperties, VkFormatProperties2, VkFormatProperties3, VkImageFormatProperties, VkImageFormatProperties2, VkPhysicalDeviceImageFormatInfo2Format capability masks and image-shape/sample/mip limits; explicit query inputs and outputs, including 64-bit feature masks
VkSparseImageFormatProperties, VkSparseImageFormatProperties2, VkSparseImageMemoryRequirements, VkSparseImageMemoryRequirements2, VkPhysicalDeviceSparseImageFormatInfo2Sparse granularity, mip-tail and format query data; these records do not allocate/bind sparse memory
VkConformanceVersion, VkPhysicalDeviceDriverProperties, VkPhysicalDeviceIDProperties, VkPhysicalDeviceToolPropertiesDriver/conformance identifiers, device/driver UUIDs and enumerated active tool metadata

Requirements and lifetime

Feature fields report support when queried and request enablement when validly chained into creation. A supported feature is not automatically enabled. Property records describe limits and capabilities and do not reserve memory, queues or descriptors. The higher-level physical_device caches only the selected core data and at most 16 queue families; these raw records permit lower-level queries independently.

Output records and their pNext extensions require writable correctly tagged storage. Native enumeration count/capacity conventions still apply. Fixed inline arrays retain value data; pointers in chain headers remain borrowed. Queries have driver costs, and these plain records themselves perform no allocation or synchronization.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkMemoryAllocateInfo

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes Vulkan resource creation, memory requirements, allocation and binding.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkBufferCreateInfo, VkBufferViewCreateInfo, VkImageCreateInfo, VkImageViewCreateInfoResource shape, usage, sharing, format and image-subresource/view creation inputs
VkMemoryAllocateInfo, VkMemoryAllocateFlagsInfo, VkMemoryRequirements, VkMemoryRequirements2, VkMappedMemoryRangeAllocation size/type/flags, queried size/alignment/type compatibility and explicit cache-maintenance ranges
VkBufferMemoryRequirementsInfo2, VkImageMemoryRequirementsInfo2, VkImageSparseMemoryRequirementsInfo2, VkDeviceBufferMemoryRequirements, VkDeviceImageMemoryRequirementsResource-handle or prospective-create-info inputs for memory-requirement queries
VkMemoryDedicatedRequirements, VkMemoryDedicatedAllocateInfoDedicated-allocation preference/requirement outputs and explicit image/buffer association input
VkBindBufferMemoryInfo, VkBindImageMemoryInfoResource, memory handle and byte offset for batched binding
VkSubresourceLayout, VkImagePlaneMemoryRequirementsInfo, VkBindImagePlaneMemoryInfoLinear-image offset/row/array/depth pitches and explicit disjoint image-plane selection
VkImageViewUsageCreateInfo, VkImageStencilUsageCreateInfo, VkImageFormatListCreateInfoView/stencil usage restrictions and an allowed view-format array
VkBufferDeviceAddressInfo, VkBufferOpaqueCaptureAddressCreateInfo, VkMemoryOpaqueCaptureAddressAllocateInfo, VkDeviceMemoryOpaqueCaptureAddressInfoDevice-address/capture-replay query and creation-chain records; enablement and allocation flags remain explicit
VkSamplerYcbcrConversionCreateInfo, VkSamplerYcbcrConversionInfo, VkSamplerYcbcrConversionImageFormatProperties, VkSamplerReductionModeCreateInfoYCbCr model/range/chroma/component conversion creation/reference data, descriptor multiplier output and reduction-mode selection

Requirements and lifetime

Resource creation and memory allocation are separate. memoryTypeBits, alignment, allocation size, usage flags and enabled feature constraints must agree before binding. Raw records do not acquire ownership or automatically add an allocation chain. Cache-maintenance ranges must satisfy noncoherent-atom alignment and mapping requirements.

Image initial-layout fields do not execute layout transitions. Linear pitches and returned device addresses describe native storage rather than a micron container. Count/pointer fields, resource handles and conversion references are borrowed under the called API's lifetime rules. These records do not insert barriers, wait for completion or release memory.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkDescriptorSetLayoutCreateInfo

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes descriptor layout, pool capacity, allocation and update operations.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkDescriptorSetLayoutBinding, VkDescriptorSetLayoutCreateInfo, VkDescriptorSetLayoutSupportBinding/type/count/stage/immutable-sampler layout inputs and a support-query output
VkDescriptorPoolSize, VkDescriptorPoolCreateInfo, VkDescriptorSetAllocateInfoPer-type descriptor counts, maximum set count/pool flags and layouts for set allocation
VkDescriptorBufferInfo, VkDescriptorImageInfo, VkWriteDescriptorSet, VkCopyDescriptorSetBuffer ranges, image-view/sampler/layout payloads and destination/source descriptor updates
VkDescriptorUpdateTemplateEntry, VkDescriptorUpdateTemplateCreateInfoTyped descriptor-update entries with caller-data byte offset/stride and compatible layout/set selection
VkDescriptorSetLayoutBindingFlagsCreateInfo, VkDescriptorSetVariableDescriptorCountAllocateInfo, VkDescriptorSetVariableDescriptorCountLayoutSupportBinding flags, requested variable counts and variable-count support outputs
VkWriteDescriptorSetInlineUniformBlock, VkDescriptorPoolInlineUniformBlockCreateInfoInline uniform byte data and pool inline-binding capacity extensions

Requirements and lifetime

The descriptorType determines which payload pointer a write consumes; these plain records do not provide a tagged owning variant. Binding arrays, immutable samplers, update data and count arrays are borrowed. Explicit descriptor indexing/inline-uniform features and native limits apply even when the corresponding record type is declared.

Allocation records identify a pool whose reset/destruction invalidates its sets. Update records do not extend the lifetime of buffers, views, samplers or their memory. Templates describe how the driver reads caller data; offset and stride do not allocate or bounds-check that storage.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes shader specialization and complete graphics/compute pipeline creation state.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkShaderModuleCreateInfo, VkSpecializationMapEntry, VkSpecializationInfo, VkPipelineShaderStageCreateInfo, VkComputePipelineCreateInfoSPIR-V byte range, specialization constant map/data, stage/entry-point selection and compute-pipeline input
VkVertexInputBindingDescription, VkVertexInputAttributeDescription, VkPipelineVertexInputStateCreateInfo, VkPipelineInputAssemblyStateCreateInfo, VkPipelineTessellationStateCreateInfoVertex binding/attribute layouts, topology/restart and tessellation patch size
VkPipelineViewportStateCreateInfo, VkPipelineRasterizationStateCreateInfo, VkPipelineMultisampleStateCreateInfoViewport/scissor arrays, rasterization state and sample count/masks
VkStencilOpState, VkPipelineDepthStencilStateCreateInfo, VkPipelineColorBlendAttachmentState, VkPipelineColorBlendStateCreateInfo, VkPipelineDynamicStateCreateInfoDepth/stencil tests and operations, per-attachment blending/color masks and dynamically supplied state selectors
VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo, VkPipelineLayoutCreateInfo, VkPushConstantRangeGraphics state graph, render-pass/layout compatibility and descriptor/push-constant interface
VkPipelineCacheCreateInfo, VkPipelineCacheHeaderVersionOne, VkPipelineCreationFeedback, VkPipelineCreationFeedbackCreateInfoInitial cache bytes/header, native compilation feedback and writable feedback arrays
VkPipelineTessellationDomainOriginStateCreateInfo, VkPipelineShaderStageRequiredSubgroupSizeCreateInfo, VkPipelineRenderingCreateInfoTessellation origin, explicit stage subgroup size and dynamic-rendering attachment format compatibility
VkSamplerCreateInfoFiltering/addressing/LOD/comparison/anisotropy sampler creation fields

Requirements and lifetime

Pipeline descriptions are graphs of borrowed pointers, with explicit array counts, entry-point strings, specialization bytes and native handle dependencies. They do not supply missing state, infer layout compatibility or compile source text before the native call. Some states may be supplied dynamically only when enabled and permitted for the selected creation path.

codeSize and specialization data sizes are byte counts; offsets/strides must address the supplied storage correctly. Feedback records are writable outputs and do not themselves measure or optimize anything. Cache bytes require a compatible driver/device format. Pipeline creation cost and allocation are native implementation costs, not a fixed function of the C++ record size.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkCommandBufferBeginInfo

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes command allocation/recording, memory dependencies, transfers, submission and synchronization.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkCommandPoolCreateInfo, VkCommandBufferAllocateInfo, VkCommandBufferInheritanceInfo, VkCommandBufferBeginInfoQueue-family pool selection, allocation count/level, inherited rendering/query state and begin flags
VkDrawIndirectCommand, VkDrawIndexedIndirectCommand, VkDispatchIndirectCommandPacked indirect argument records consumed from GPU buffers by the corresponding draw/dispatch command
VkBufferCopy, VkImageCopy, VkImageBlit, VkBufferImageCopy, VkImageResolveLegacy transfer regions with byte ranges or explicitly selected image subresources/offsets/extents
VkBufferCopy2, VkImageCopy2, VkImageBlit2, VkBufferImageCopy2, VkImageResolve2, VkCopyBufferInfo2, VkCopyImageInfo2, VkBlitImageInfo2, VkCopyBufferToImageInfo2, VkCopyImageToBufferInfo2, VkResolveImageInfo2Extensible transfer regions and command inputs for the corresponding version-2 copy/blit/resolve API
VkMemoryBarrier, VkBufferMemoryBarrier, VkImageMemoryBarrier, VkMemoryBarrier2, VkBufferMemoryBarrier2, VkImageMemoryBarrier2, VkDependencyInfoExplicit access/stage dependencies, resource ranges/layouts and queue-family ownership; synchronization2 carries 64-bit stage/access masks
VkSubmitInfo, VkSubmitInfo2, VkSemaphoreSubmitInfo, VkCommandBufferSubmitInfo, VkProtectedSubmitInfoCommand-buffer and semaphore dependency batches, timeline values/stages/device masks and protected-submit selection
VkFenceCreateInfo, VkSemaphoreCreateInfo, VkEventCreateInfo, VkQueryPoolCreateInfoInitial fence state, semaphore/event creation flags and query type/count/statistics selection
VkSemaphoreTypeCreateInfo, VkTimelineSemaphoreSubmitInfo, VkSemaphoreWaitInfo, VkSemaphoreSignalInfoBinary/timeline kind and initial value, submit value arrays, host wait targets and host signal target
VkQueueFamilyCheckpointProperties2NV, VkCheckpointData2NVNV diagnostic checkpoint stage capabilities and returned stage/borrowed marker data

Requirements and lifetime

Description does not execute an operation: barriers must be recorded, command buffers ended/submitted and completion observed explicitly. Access masks, pipeline stages, layouts, ownership transfers and wait/signal values must agree with native synchronization rules. The synchronization2 KHR spellings are aliases of the corresponding selected core record/mask types.

Input arrays/regions are borrowed for the native call; command buffers and referenced resources must remain usable for outstanding submitted work. Indirect records describe buffer contents, not an owned CPU queue. Query output layout is determined by flags and stride. No structure here waits, retains a resource owner or allocates a result container.

micron::gfx::vk::VkRenderPassCreateInfo

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes render attachments, subpasses, framebuffer bindings and dynamic rendering.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkAttachmentDescription, VkAttachmentReference, VkSubpassDescription, VkSubpassDependency, VkRenderPassCreateInfo, VkRenderPassBeginInfoLegacy attachment formats/load/store/layouts, subpass attachment indices, dependencies and render-area/clear inputs
VkAttachmentDescription2, VkAttachmentReference2, VkSubpassDescription2, VkSubpassDependency2, VkRenderPassCreateInfo2, VkSubpassBeginInfo, VkSubpassEndInfoExtensible render-pass descriptions and explicit subpass command inputs
VkRenderPassMultiviewCreateInfo, VkInputAttachmentAspectReference, VkRenderPassInputAttachmentAspectCreateInfo, VkSubpassDescriptionDepthStencilResolveView/correlation masks, input-attachment aspects and depth/stencil resolve modes/attachments
VkAttachmentReferenceStencilLayout, VkAttachmentDescriptionStencilLayoutSeparate stencil layout fields for attachment reference and load/store description chains
VkFramebufferCreateInfo, VkFramebufferAttachmentsCreateInfo, VkFramebufferAttachmentImageInfo, VkRenderPassAttachmentBeginInfoOrdinary framebuffer view array or imageless attachment constraints and begin-time views
VkRenderingInfo, VkRenderingAttachmentInfo, VkCommandBufferInheritanceRenderingInfoDynamic-rendering area/layers/attachments and load/store/resolve/clear data; inherited formats and samples for secondary recording

Requirements and lifetime

Attachment indices are indices into the applicable creation arrays, not object handles. Raw attachment/layout/subpass fields are forwarded without validation by the wrapper. Multiview, dynamic rendering, separate depth/stencil layouts and imageless framebuffers require their applicable supported/enabled features and complete compatible records.

Framebuffer and rendering records borrow image views and native handles. Clear-value unions are interpreted from the attachment format/use. Array storage must satisfy the native call lifetime; referenced GPU resources must outlive execution. These records do not resize attachments, insert missing dependencies or choose safe layout transitions automatically.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkSwapchainCreateInfoKHR

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes native window-system surface, swapchain, acquisition and presentation operations.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkWaylandSurfaceCreateInfoKHR, VkXlibSurfaceCreateInfoKHRBorrowed native Wayland display/surface or XDisplay/window identifiers for surface creation
VkSurfaceCapabilitiesKHR, VkSurfaceFormatKHRSurface image-count/extent/usage/transform/alpha capabilities and a supported format/color-space pair
VkSwapchainCreateInfoKHR, VkPresentInfoKHR, VkAcquireNextImageInfoKHRPresentation image configuration, wait semaphores and chain/image indices; acquisition timeout/signal/device-mask input
VkImageSwapchainCreateInfoKHR, VkBindImageMemorySwapchainInfoKHRSwapchain image association for image creation and memory-binding chains

Requirements and lifetime

A declared format, color space or present-mode value does not establish surface support. Query capabilities and supported choices before filling a raw creation record. Zero/maximal extents and special queue-family values have native meanings rather than implicit C++ bounds checking.

Surface records borrow native window/display objects, and swapchain/presentation records borrow Vulkan surface/queue/synchronization handles. Acquisition/presentation can return timeout, suboptimal or out-of-date statuses; callers must distinguish those results before using an index or rebuilding. Record destruction performs no native release or GPU wait.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkExternalMemoryProperties

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes external-handle capabilities, device groups and sparse memory binding.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkExternalMemoryProperties, VkPhysicalDeviceExternalImageFormatInfo, VkExternalImageFormatProperties, VkPhysicalDeviceExternalBufferInfo, VkExternalBufferPropertiesExternal memory handle-type query input and import/export/dedicated capability outputs
VkExternalMemoryImageCreateInfo, VkExternalMemoryBufferCreateInfo, VkExportMemoryAllocateInfoRequested external memory handle types for resources and allocations
VkPhysicalDeviceExternalSemaphoreInfo, VkExternalSemaphoreProperties, VkExportSemaphoreCreateInfo, VkPhysicalDeviceExternalFenceInfo, VkExternalFenceProperties, VkExportFenceCreateInfoExternal semaphore/fence handle compatibility queries and export creation chains
VkPhysicalDeviceGroupProperties, VkDeviceGroupDeviceCreateInfo, VkBindBufferMemoryDeviceGroupInfo, VkBindImageMemoryDeviceGroupInfoGroup membership/device creation and device-index/split-region binding arrays
VkDeviceGroupRenderPassBeginInfo, VkDeviceGroupCommandBufferBeginInfo, VkDeviceGroupSubmitInfo, VkDeviceGroupBindSparseInfoDevice masks, per-device render regions and submit/bind device selections
VkDeviceGroupPresentCapabilitiesKHR, VkDeviceGroupPresentInfoKHR, VkDeviceGroupSwapchainCreateInfoKHRGroup presentation masks/modes and swapchain/present group selection
VkSparseMemoryBind, VkSparseImageMemoryBind, VkSparseBufferMemoryBindInfo, VkSparseImageOpaqueMemoryBindInfo, VkSparseImageMemoryBindInfo, VkBindSparseInfoExplicit sparse memory ranges/image blocks and queue-binding batches with semaphore dependencies

Requirements and lifetime

These are raw capability and request records, not owners of file descriptors, OS handles, physical devices or memory pages. External handle-type enumerators include values for other platforms; their declaration does not implement a platform backend. The selected local dispatch table does not provide a complete import/export convenience layer for every represented handle type.

Device-group and sparse records borrow arrays and native handles. Correct masks, compatible memory types, granularity, enabled features and queue synchronization remain caller requirements. No local allocator, sparse residency tracker, ownership graph or automatic external-handle close is attached to these records.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkDebugUtilsMessengerCallbackDataEXT

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Describes debug-utils messages, labels, object names/tags and messenger creation.

Types and roles

TypesRole
VkDebugUtilsObjectNameInfoEXT, VkDebugUtilsObjectTagInfoEXTObject category plus 64-bit raw handle and borrowed name or arbitrary byte-tag payload
VkDebugUtilsLabelEXTBorrowed label name and four inline color components
VkDebugUtilsMessengerCreateInfoEXT, VkDebugUtilsMessengerCallbackDataEXTSeverity/type filters, callback/user data and borrowed message/label/object arrays supplied to callbacks
VkAllocationCallbacksBorrowed host allocate/reallocate/free and internal-notification callbacks plus user data

Requirements and lifetime

Callback data and pointed-to message/array storage have the native callback lifetime. A listener that keeps data must copy the applicable values into its own storage. Naming/tagging records do not retain their target object or establish that a handle matches the supplied VkObjectType.

VkAllocationCallbacks concerns host allocations, distinct from GPU allocation records. Callback user data must remain valid while the driver may invoke it, and each native object must be destroyed with an allocator association compatible with its creation. These plain records perform no allocation, logging or synchronization themselves.

Callback signature aliases

PFN_vkAllocationFunction, PFN_vkReallocationFunction and PFN_vkFreeFunction define host allocation callbacks. PFN_vkInternalAllocationNotification and PFN_vkInternalFreeNotification report internal driver allocation events; they do not provide the driver with those blocks. PFN_vkDebugUtilsMessengerCallbackEXT receives severity, type, borrowed callback data and user data and returns VkBool32. PFN_vkVoidFunction is an untyped command pointer returned by procedure lookup and must be cast to the correct actual signature before use.

Related types and selection guide.

VkPhysicalDeviceSamplerYcbcrConversionFeatures, VkPhysicalDeviceSamplerFilterMinmaxProperties, VkPhysicalDeviceTextureCompressionASTCHDRFeatures, VkPhysicalDeviceImageRobustnessFeaturesYCbCr conversion, min/max filtering, ASTC HDR and robust image access support

Requirements and lifetime

Each individual record contains sType, pNext and the named Boolean or numeric outputs. Feature Booleans must be queried and then explicitly requested in a permitted device-creation chain; property records are query outputs, not enablement requests. Version-grouped Vulkan11/12/13 feature records overlap the corresponding individual facilities and do not remove native chain-compatibility requirements.

The convenience device_hints forwards core plus a Vulkan13 feature record and clears that record's pNext. It does not preserve an arbitrary chain attached there. A type declaration therefore does not establish a complete convenience enablement path. The older VariablePointer/ShaderDrawParameter spellings and synchronization2 KHR spellings alias their corresponding selected core types rather than owning separate state.

Related types and selection guide.

VkColorSpaceKHR, VkPresentModeKHR, VkSurfaceTransformFlagBitsKHR, VkCompositeAlphaFlagBitsKHR, VkSwapchainCreateFlagBitsKHR, VkDeviceGroupPresentModeFlagBitsKHRSurface color space/presentation policy, display transform, alpha composition and swapchain/group presentation options
VkSparseMemoryBindFlagBits, VkSparseImageFormatFlagBits, VkPeerMemoryFeatureFlagBits, VkExternalMemoryHandleTypeFlagBits, VkExternalMemoryFeatureFlagBits, VkExternalSemaphoreHandleTypeFlagBits, VkExternalSemaphoreFeatureFlagBits, VkSemaphoreImportFlagBits, VkExternalFenceHandleTypeFlagBits, VkExternalFenceFeatureFlagBits, VkFenceImportFlagBitsSparse binding/format properties, peer-device operations and external handle import/export/capability bits
VkSystemAllocationScope, VkInternalAllocationType, VkDebugUtilsMessageSeverityFlagBitsEXT, VkDebugUtilsMessageTypeFlagBitsEXT, VkShaderFloatControlsIndependenceHost allocation scope/type, debug severity/category and floating-point control independence

Requirements and lifetime

These enum classes preserve typed selector/bit domains. Most non-bit selectors use i32, ordinary bit enums use u32, and AccessFlagBits2/PipelineStageFlagBits2/FormatFeatureFlagBits2 use u64. The generated VK_* names are constexpr aliases into the corresponding enum class, not untyped preprocessor integers. Use flags<BitType> and its associated aliases for combined masks.

Represented tokens include selected extension values and empty reserved flag domains. Their presence does not enable an extension, make a reserved nonzero bit valid or guarantee a device/format capability. A layout enum describes the promised native layout and does not transition an image. Present-mode values select a presentation policy and do not bound frame latency in this wrapper.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkResult

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Carries Vulkan success, progress and error status values as a signed enum.

Results and wrapper conventions

SUCCESS is zero. NOT_READY, TIMEOUT, EVENT_SET, EVENT_RESET, INCOMPLETE, PIPELINE_COMPILE_REQUIRED and SUBOPTIMAL_KHR are distinct nonzero statuses; negative values identify the declared host/device allocation, initialization, lost-device, feature/extension, pool, surface and other errors. A nonnegative result is not necessarily completion of the operation expected by a particular wrapper.

check_vk accepts only SUCCESS and throws for every other result, including positive progress/status values. Creation/enumeration wrappers using it can therefore reject INCOMPLETE or PIPELINE_COMPILE_REQUIRED. Fence/timeline waits explicitly translate TIMEOUT to false, while acquisition, presentation and query-result operations expose raw VkResult so the caller can handle their supported status set.

Status helpers that return a Boolean or zero can collapse errors with ordinary not-ready/zero outcomes; their type articles identify those cases. The signed-byte-count -errno convention used by operating-system I/O is not the result convention for raw Vulkan calls.

Related types and selection guide.

micron::gfx::vk::VkBool32

Defined in header <micron/gfx/vk/__bits/__vk_types.hpp>.

Supplies Vulkan scalar carriers, opaque native handles and local platform type aliases.

Scalar and handle families

FamilyRepresentation and meaning
VkBool32, VkSampleMasku32 native Boolean and sample-mask fields; a C++ bool has a different representation
VkDeviceSize, VkDeviceAddressu64 byte size/offset and device address carriers; neither owns or validates memory
VkInstance, VkPhysicalDevice, VkDevice, VkQueue, VkCommandBufferOpaque pointer handle aliases for dispatchable native objects
Memory, pool, buffer/view, image/view, shader, pipeline/cache/layout, sampler, descriptor, sync, query, render, private-data, surface/swapchain and debug-messenger handlesOpaque pointer handle aliases in this generated header, including its non-dispatchable handle types
Corresponding Vk*_T declarationsIncomplete object tags used only to make handle pointer types distinct; no concrete C++ storage layout or destructor is supplied
Display, Window, wl_display, wl_surfaceAliases to the local platform X11/Wayland ABI types; native objects remain borrowed

ABI limit and ownership

The generated header represents every handle as an opaque pointer without a 32-bit non-dispatchable-handle branch. On a 32-bit target those carriers are pointer-sized, whereas the native non-dispatchable ABI requires a 64-bit carrier. This declaration set therefore does not supply a correct general 32-bit Vulkan handle ABI. The Linux-only preprocessor gate does not by itself resolve that width mismatch.

Copying any raw handle aliases the same native object and does not increment an ownership count. Use a dedicated resource wrapper or unique/shared with the matching deleter when ownership is required. Parent-instance/device/pool lifetime, external synchronization and GPU completion remain independent obligations.

Native handle inventory

TypesNative object family
VkInstance, VkPhysicalDevice, VkDevice, VkQueue, VkCommandBufferDispatchable instance/device/queue/command objects
VkDeviceMemory, VkCommandPool, VkDescriptorSet, VkDescriptorSetLayout, VkDescriptorPoolGPU allocation and command/descriptor storage or interfaces
VkBuffer, VkBufferView, VkImage, VkImageView, VkSampler, VkSamplerYcbcrConversionResource storage and interpretation/sampling objects
VkShaderModule, VkPipeline, VkPipelineLayout, VkFramebuffer, VkRenderPass, VkPipelineCache, VkDescriptorUpdateTemplate, VkPrivateDataSlotShader/pipeline/rendering interfaces, cached data and private-data slots
VkFence, VkSemaphore, VkEvent, VkQueryPoolNative synchronization and query objects

Semantics

VkFlags is u32 and VkFlags64 is u64. flags<BitEnum> aliases preserve the corresponding enum domain while retaining its underlying mask width. Aliases whose carrier is VkFlags are raw masks, including reserved fields with no currently selected bits. Mask construction does not check device support or make a reserved bit valid. All aliases store values inline and own no resource.

The combined-bit meaning follows the corresponding create-info, access/stage, format, descriptor, resource, synchronization, surface or external-handle field. The flags article describes typed bitwise operators and complement. A flags alias is neither an enabled-feature set nor a dynamically sized bit container.

Related types and selection guide.
